<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the finite-volume continuum-random-tree prediction for critical Fortuin–Kasteleyn planar maps at every fixed <i>q</i> &gt; 4. After rescaling graph distances by a constant times <i>n</i><sup>−1/2</sup>, an <i>n</i>-edge map with normalized degree measure converges to the Brownian continuum random tree in the Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology. The convergence holds through all positive integer sizes.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed real $q>4$, there is a deterministic constant $c(q)\in(0,\infty)$ such that, as $n\to\infty$ through all positive integers, $$\big(V(M_n),c(q)n^{-1/2}d_n,\mu_n\big) \ \Longrightarrow\ (\mathcal T_e,d_e,\mu_e)$$ in the Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology on compact metric probability spaces modulo measure-preserving isometry.
  - Explanation: Critical finite Fortuin–Kasteleyn map distances and degree mass converge to the normalized continuum random tree.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-word-reduction)): Feed a finite word an arbitrary older list of burgers. Every match between two letters of the word is the same as when the word is read from an empty supply. The orders unfilled in the latter evaluation act on the older list in their original chronology, and the surviving burgers of the word remain above the surviving older burgers. In particular, finite words may be replaced by their reductions when concatenating them. Once a suffix has a surviving burger, its freshest survivor is unchanged by prepending further letters.
  - Explanation: Older supply preserves internal matches and permits reduction before concatenation.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-word-identity)): For every integer $n\ge0$, an independent word of length $2n$ satisfies $$\label{word:identity-equation} \mathbb P(\text{the word is empty-reducing})=a_nu^n.$$
  - Explanation: Total finite map weight equals an empty-reduction probability after exponential normalization.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-word-catalan)): For every $n\ge0$, $$\label{word:catalan-equation} a_nu^n\le \frac{1}{4^n(n+1)}\binom{2n}{n} =O\bigl((n+1)^{-3/2}\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Forgetting burger types bounds complete matching by a simple-walk excursion probability.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-word-drift)): The stopping time $J$ is almost surely finite, and $$\label{word:drift-equation} \mathbb EK\le\frac{1}{2p}<1.$$
  - Explanation: The backward survivor exists almost surely and its residual order count has strict drift.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-ladder-lower)): There is $c>0$ such that, for all real $x<1$ sufficiently close to $1$, $$\sum_{n\ge1} n a_n u^n x^n\ge c(1-x)^{-1/2}. \label{ladder:coefficient-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Successfully matched seam intervals force a square-root derivative lower bound.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ladder-averaging)): Let $(Z_i)_{i\in\mathbb Z}$ be iid, and let $Y_i$ be the translates of an integrable measurable function of the entire sequence $(Z_i)_{i\in\mathbb Z}$. Then the averages of $Y_i$ in each index direction converge almost surely to $\mathbb EY_0$. In particular, a shift-covariant set of boundaries having positive probability at boundary $0$ has positive limiting density in both directions. If $0$ belongs to this set, the last such boundary before $k$ is $k-o(k)$, and the first one after $-k$ is $-k+o(k)$.
  - Explanation: Stationary observations of independent pieces have limiting averages in both directions.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ladder-cuts)): Each fixed boundary has positive probability of being an $L$-cut and positive probability of being an $R$-cut, in the respective piece laws. Consequently both cut sets have positive limiting densities in both index directions.
  - Explanation: Left and right clean-reduction cuts have positive probability and positive density.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ladder-matching)): Let a burger stack and an order word both have length $h$. Suppose that, for every $1\le k\le h$ and each type $a$, the number of fixed type-$a$ orders among the terminal $k$ orders is at most the number of type-$a$ burgers among the original bottom $k$ burgers. Then the greedy inventory rule matches all orders and leaves no burgers.
  - Explanation: Bottom supply dominating terminal fixed demand ensures greedy matching succeeds.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ladder-frequencies)): Almost surely, each burger type has frequency $1/2$ in the stationary $L$-output. The stationary $R$-output has almost-sure frequencies $(1-\rho)/2,(1-\rho)/2,\rho$ for the two fixed types and the flexible orders, respectively, for a deterministic $\rho>0$.* *On the event that $0$ is an $L$-cut, let $B_a(h,k)$ count type-$a$ burgers among the bottom $k$ burgers of the reduction of the $h$ pieces starting at $0$, where $1\le k\le h$. Then, almost surely on this event, $$\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{h\ge k} \left\|\frac{B_a(h,k)}k-\frac12\right\|=0, \qquad a=1,2. \label{ladder:left-counts}$$ On the event that $0$ is an $R$-cut, let $C_a(h,k)$ count fixed orders of type $a$ among the terminal $k$ residual orders of the $h$ pieces ending at $0$. Then, almost surely on this event, $$\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{h\ge k} \left\|\frac{C_a(h,k)}k-\frac{1-\rho}{2}\right\|=0, \qquad a=1,2. \label{ladder:right-counts}$$
  - Explanation: Stationary cut outputs have balanced types and positive flexible-order frequency.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ladder-uniform)): There is $\delta>0$, depending only on $p$, such that for every integer $h\ge1$ the seam interval formed by the backwards hit of $+h$ and the forwards hit of $-h$ reduces to the empty word with probability at least $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Seam matching succeeds with a positive lower bound independent of ladder height.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-block-decomposition)): Every nontrivial rooted planar map is uniquely obtained from a rooted block by inserting a rooted map, possibly trivial, in each of its corners. A nonempty insert is attached at the origin of its root dart. Moreover, $a$ is multiplicative when two maps are joined at one vertex. Consequently, with $$T(z)=\sum_{n\ge0}a_nz^n,\qquad \Phi(y)=1+\sum_{m\ge1}b_my^m,$$ there is an identity of formal power series $$\label{block:equation} T(z)=\Phi\big(zT(z)^2\big).$$
  - Explanation: Rooted corner insertion is unique and weights multiply across attachment vertices.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-block-critical)): Suppose $T$ and $\Phi$ have nonnegative coefficients and constant term $1$, and satisfy <a href="#block:equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="block:equation">[block:equation]</a>. Let $u>0$ satisfy $T(u)<\infty$, and suppose that, for some $c>0$, $$\label{block:lower-assumption} \sum_{n\ge1}na_nu^nx^n\ge c(1-x)^{-1/2} \qquad\text{for all $x<1$ sufficiently close to $1$}.$$ Put $\tau=T(u)$ and $v=u\tau^2$. Then $$\label{block:critical-equations} \Phi(v)=\tau,\qquad 2v\Phi'(v)=\Phi(v),\qquad \Phi''(v)<\infty,$$ where endpoint derivatives denote the corresponding nonnegative series.
  - Explanation: The endpoint inverse criterion forces criticality and finite offspring variance.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-block-finite-law)): The marked tree conditioned to have $N=2n+1$ nodes produces exactly the map marginal of <a href="#eq:finite-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-law">[eq:finite-law]</a>. Every such size is attainable.
  - Explanation: Size conditioning gives exactly the finite map marginal at every attainable map size.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tree-excursion)): Under <a href="#tree:assumptions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tree:assumptions">[tree:assumptions]</a>, $$\left(\frac{W_{Nt}}{\sigma\sqrt N}, \frac{\sigma H_{Nt}}{2\sqrt N}\right)_{0\leq t\leq1} \ \Longrightarrow\ (e(t),e(t))_{0\leq t\leq1} \quad\hbox{in }C([0,1],\mathbb R^2), \label{tree:walk-limit}$$ where $e$ is a standard normalized Brownian excursion.
  - Explanation: The conditioned exploration walk and height converge jointly to the normalized excursion.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-tree-maxdegree)): We have $$\max_v H(v)=O_{\mathbb P}(\sqrt N),\qquad \max_v W(v)=O_{\mathbb P}(\sqrt N),\qquad \Delta_N=o_{\mathbb P}(\sqrt N), \label{tree:degree-bound}$$ and $$\max_v\|W(v)-\nu H(v)\|=o_{\mathbb P}(\sqrt N). \label{tree:walk-height-estimate}$$
  - Explanation: Maximal degrees vanish on the scaling of heights and the walk approximates scaled height uniformly.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tree-conditioning)): As $N$ tends to infinity through odd integers, $$\pi_N:=\mathbb P(\|\mathcal T\|=N) \sim\frac{2}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi}}N^{-3/2}. \label{tree:size-probability}$$
  - Explanation: Total tree size probability has a lattice-compatible asymptotic on odd sizes.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tree-bounded-paths)): For every fixed bounded measurable real label $b$, $$\max_{v\in\mathcal T_N}\|B_b(v)-\mu_bH(v)\| =o_{\mathbb P}(\sqrt N). \label{tree:bounded-sums}$$
  - Explanation: Bounded marked path sums approximate spine means uniformly across the tree.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tree-paths)): Let $b=b(k,i,A)$ be a fixed measurable label with $0\leq b\leq k$. Then $\mu_b<\infty$ and $$\max_{v\in\mathcal T_N}\|B_b(v)-\mu_bH(v)\|=o_{\mathbb P}(\sqrt N), \qquad \max_{v\in\mathcal T_N} \left\|B_b(v)-\frac{\mu_b}{\nu}W(v)\right\|=o_{\mathbb P}(\sqrt N). \label{tree:marked-sums}$$
  - Explanation: Labels bounded by offspring count admit uniform path laws with finite offspring variance.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-tree-lca)): If $j\leq j'$ and $v_*$ is the lowest common ancestor of $v_j,v_{j'}$, then $$W(v_*)\leq\min_{j\leq r\leq j'}W_r\leq W(v_*)+\Delta_N. \label{tree:lca-walk}$$
  - Explanation: An interval walk minimum approximates the common ancestor walk value.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-metric-poles)): Let $w_*$ be the last common ancestor of two tree nodes $w,w'$. If $\Delta$ is the maximum offspring number, then $$\left\|d_{M_n}\big(\pi(w),\pi(w')\big) -\big(G(w)+G(w')-2G(w_*)\big)\right\|\le2\Delta.$$ The map $\pi$ from tree nodes to map vertices is onto.
  - Explanation: Pole distances differ from accumulated block distances by a maximal-offspring error.
- **C22** (theorem, [source](#stmt-metric-measure)): The uniform probability measure on the $N-1=2n$ nonroot tree nodes, pushed forward by $\pi$, is exactly $\mu_n$.
  - Explanation: Uniform nonroot tree-node mass pushes exactly to the map degree measure.
- **C23** (bound, [source](#stmt-metric-ghp)): Suppose a correspondence between compact metric probability spaces has distortion at most $\varepsilon>0$, and a coupling of their probability measures is supported on the correspondence. Their Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov distance is at most $\varepsilon$. If one marginal of that coupling differs from the desired measure by total variation at most $\eta$, the bound is $\varepsilon+\eta$.
  - Explanation: Correspondence distortion and coupling mass error bound the metric probability-space distance.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **rooted planar map** ([source](#eq:finite-law)): A *rooted planar map* is a connected graph embedded in an oriented sphere, up to orientation-preserving homeomorphism, with a distinguished oriented edge, or *dart*.
- **spanning component count** ([source](#eq:finite-law)): For an edge subset $A\subseteq E(M)$, let $k_M(A)$ be the number of components of the spanning graph $(V(M),A)$, counting isolated vertices.
- **excursion pseudometric** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $s,t\in[0,1]$, define $$d_e(s,t)=e(s)+e(t)- 2\min_{r\in[s\wedge t,s\vee t]}e(r).$$
- **Brownian continuum random tree** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The quotient by $d_e=0$, equipped with its induced metric and the pushforward $\mu_e$ of Lebesgue probability measure, is denoted $(\mathcal T_e,d_e,\mu_e)$.
- **finite word reduction** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The *reduction* of a finite word is its list of unfilled orders, followed by its list of surviving burgers, with each list retaining its original chronology.
- **empty-reducing word** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A word is *empty-reducing* if both lists are empty.
- **left ladder cut** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): For a two-sided iid sequence of $L$-pieces, a boundary $a$ is an *$L$-cut* if, for every $b>a$, the word between $a$ and $b$ has no unfilled orders when evaluated from an empty supply.
- **right ladder cut** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): For a two-sided iid sequence of $R$-pieces, a boundary $b$ is an *$R$-cut* if, for every $a<b$, the word between $a$ and $b$ leaves no surviving burgers.
- **corner** ([source](#block:section)): A *corner* of a nonempty map is a sector between successive darts at a vertex.
- **blocks** ([source](#pm-h-25)): Our nontrivial *blocks* are the rooted one-loop map and the rooted nonempty connected loopless maps with at least two vertices and no separating vertex.
- **pole** ([source](#pm-h-27)): We call the origin of a block’s root dart its *pole*; the pole of a trivial insert is its attachment vertex.
- **correspondence** ([source](#pm-h-34)): A *correspondence* between $X$ and $Y$ is a relation $\mathcal R\subseteq X\times Y$ projecting onto both spaces.

        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **S1** [claim](#stmt-word-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-word-identity): Inventory bijection and embedding activity expansion. -> Exact finite word identity.
- **S2** [claim](#stmt-word-catalan) / [proof](#proof-stmt-word-catalan): Reflection after forgetting burger types. -> Endpoint finiteness of the map series.
- **S3** [claim](#stmt-word-drift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-word-drift): Bounded stopping and stationary covariance. -> Strict mean drift.
- **S4** [claim](#stmt-ladder-uniform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ladder-uniform): Stationary cuts and terminal-demand comparison. -> Uniform seam matching probability.
- **S5** [claim](#stmt-ladder-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ladder-uniform-2): Count distinct seam intervals with hitting-time control. -> Square-root coefficient derivative lower bound.
- **S6** [claim](#stmt-block-critical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-critical): Monotone series substitution and quadratic inverse analysis. -> Critical block law with finite offspring variance.
- **S7** [claim](#stmt-block-finite-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-finite-law): Multiply block factors under size conditioning. -> Exact finite map marginal for every integer edge size.
- **S8** [claim](#stmt-tree-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tree-paths): Spine estimates, reflected exploration and truncation. -> Uniform accumulated path labels.
- **S9** [claim](#stmt-metric-measure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-metric-measure): Count parent-block corners and incident darts. -> Exact degree-mass pushforward.
- **S10** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-metric-ghp-2): Uniform metric comparison and probability coupling. -> Compact metric probability-space convergence.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-matching**: The greedy inventory rule matches all orders and leaves no burgers under the bottom-supply and terminal-demand inequalities.
- **I-paths**: Uniform ancestral path estimates hold for fixed measurable nonnegative labels bounded by the offspring number under the stated conditioned-tree assumptions.
- **I-limit**: The rescaled all-edge graph metric with degree probability measure converges to the Brownian continuum random tree for a fixed admissible cluster weight.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-uniform**: Use the scaling result uniformly while the cluster weight approaches the transition. -> The theorem does not control constants uniformly at the transition.
- **A-allgraphs**: Treat arbitrary observed graphs as having the same continuum tree limit. -> The model law, metric and probability measure have not been identified with those of the theorem.
- **A-criterion**: Reject every successfully matching word whose supply-demand inequalities fail. -> The lemma states a sufficient criterion and gives no necessity claim.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The bottom-supply and terminal-demand lemma gives a finite constructive matching rule. The executable implements this rule; the continuum scaling limit is outside its scope.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON integer h on stdin, with 0 <= h <= 5.
# PRECONDITIONS: two burger types; orders 0 and 1 are fixed, order 2 is flexible; stacks run oldest to freshest.
# OUTPUTS: JSON records for every length-h stack/order pair, including the sufficient-condition flag and consumed original indices.
# TIME: O(6^h * h^2).
# SPACE: O(6^h * h) for the output records.
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers and python-flint fmpz counts.
# FAILURE: malformed or out-of-contract input raises an exception; an unfilled order is recorded as -1.
import sys, json, itertools
from flint import fmpz
h = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
if type(h) is not int or not 0 <= h <= 5:
    raise ValueError("invalid length")
records = []
for burgers in itertools.product(range(2), repeat=h):
    for orders in itertools.product(range(3), repeat=h):
        sufficient = True
        for k in range(1, h + 1):
            for a in range(2):
                supply = fmpz(burgers[:k].count(a))
                demand = fmpz(orders[-k:].count(a))
                sufficient = sufficient and demand <= supply
        stack = list(enumerate(burgers))
        consumed = []
        for order in orders:
            eligible = [j for j, item in enumerate(stack) if order == 2 or item[1] == order]
            if not eligible:
                consumed.append(-1)
            else:
                pos = eligible[-1]
                consumed.append(stack.pop(pos)[0])
        records.append([list(burgers), list(orders), sufficient, consumed])
print(json.dumps(records, separators=(",", ":")))
```

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

| software | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Conditional transfer to accumulated costs on random software hierarchies is possible only after establishing the stated conditioned plane Galton–Watson law and conditional mark law. No concrete software model satisfying these hypotheses is supplied. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Random-map simulations can use the theorem as an asymptotic geometric target when their samples follow the specified finite critical Fortuin–Kasteleyn law. The paper does not provide a general procedural-world generator or a finite-size approximation tolerance. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A general-purpose tooling contract is ABSENT. The source defines inventory reduction and probabilistic geometry, without a specification for a development, diagnostic, or deployment tool. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The inventory rule yields a finite deterministic word-reduction procedure. Its internal matching invariance supports composing reductions; this is separate from computing or certifying the continuum limit. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The ordered block tree is an exact representation of rooted planar maps under corner substitution. Empty insertions remain leaves, so the structure retains corner multiplicities needed for degree mass; it is not a generic graph compression guarantee. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An accelerator architecture, parallel implementation, and hardware performance contract are ABSENT. The sequential inventory rule and scaling theorem do not supply these engineering guarantees. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A cryptographic construction or adversarial security model is ABSENT. The random-map limit and matching rule establish geometric and combinatorial properties without hardness or security guarantees. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A learning objective, training procedure, and generalization guarantee are ABSENT. The stated random-tree hypotheses do not establish a model for learned or empirical network graphs. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An optimization objective or optimality guarantee is ABSENT. Greedy inventory matching serves a prescribed chronological rule; the sufficient supply-demand condition is not an optimization theorem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A communication, fault, or consensus model is ABSENT. Independence and conditioning in the branching-tree argument do not establish properties of distributed communication graphs. |
| other (probability_and_random_geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The direct domain is probability and random geometry: critical finite Fortuin–Kasteleyn planar maps converge to the Brownian continuum random tree under the stated metric and measure normalization. This gives a mathematical asymptotic target rather than an executable finite-size guarantee. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-fixed-q** (hypothesis): The map limit requires fixed $q>4$ under the stated critical Fortuin–Kasteleyn law. Blocked use: ['Transferring the map limit to another random-map ensemble or another parameter regime.']
- **L-nonuniform** (edge_case): Constants may depend on the fixed parameter $q$; no uniform statement as $q$ approaches $4$ is asserted. Blocked use: ['Using the convergence uniformly as the parameter approaches the transition.']
- **L-asymptotic** (approximation): The convergence is an asymptotic distributional statement through positive integer map sizes; a computable finite-size error bound is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Treating convergence as a certified approximation tolerance for a particular finite map.']
- **L-distance** (hypothesis): The map distance uses every edge of the map. Blocked use: ['Replacing graph distance by distance restricted to cluster edges, weighted edges, or an encoding walk without metric comparison.']
- **L-degree-mass** (hypothesis): The probability measure is normalized degree mass, with loops contributing two to degree. Blocked use: ['Substituting uniform vertex mass without a separate measure comparison.']
- **L-forgotten** (counterexample_scope): The stated convergence forgets the root and the FK decoration. Blocked use: ['Claiming joint convergence of cluster decoration or rooted structure from the metric probability-space conclusion.']
- **L-tree-law** (hypothesis): The conditioned-tree results require a plane Galton–Watson law with even nonnegative offspring, positive mass at zero and two, critical mean, and finite positive variance. Blocked use: ['Applying the conditioned-tree limit to arbitrary deterministic, directed, cyclic, or empirically observed graphs.']
- **L-attainable** (edge_case): The conditioned-tree limits run through odd node counts; this does not restrict the map theorem to odd edge counts. Blocked use: ['Conditioning the stated even-offspring tree on an unattainable size.']
- **L-marks** (hypothesis): Given offspring numbers, marks are independent and have a fixed law conditional on the number of children. Blocked use: ['Allowing dependent marks or a varying mark law without proving a replacement path estimate.']
- **L-label** (hypothesis): The unbounded path-sum result requires a fixed measurable label with $0\leq b\leq k$. Blocked use: ['Applying the unbounded path estimate to arbitrary signed costs or labels exceeding the offspring bound.']
- **L-endpoint** (hypothesis): The quadratic inverse criterion requires nonnegative coefficients, the corner-substitution identity, a finite endpoint value, and the stated coefficient lower bound. Blocked use: ['Inferring criticality and finite variance from recursive decomposition alone.']
- **L-coupling** (hypothesis): The metric comparison requires a correspondence with controlled distortion and a probability coupling supported on it. Blocked use: ['Claiming a metric-measure conclusion from a walk limit or a metric comparison without measure control.']
- **L-finite-certificate** (nonconstructive): The continuum scaling limit is not a finite constructive computation; finite word-matching checks certify only their stated finite scope. Blocked use: ['Treating exhaustive finite word or tree checks as a certificate of convergence to the Brownian continuum random tree.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 211 (`lean/docs/211.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Brownian continuum random tree limits of finite Fortuin–Kasteleyn maps above four](../../preprints/Brownian-continuum-random-tree-limits-of-finite-Fortuin-Kasteleyn-maps-above-four-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> For every fixed $q>4$, the formalization proves the Brownian continuum-random-tree limit for critical finite Fortuin–Kasteleyn planar maps. After rescaling graph distances in an $n$-edge map by a constant depending on $q$ times $n^{-1/2}$ and using normalized degree measure, the metric-measure space converges in distribution to the Brownian continuum random tree in the Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology. The convergence holds through all positive integer sizes.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-!
Finite Fortuin–Kasteleyn maps and the Brownian continuum random tree.

All combinatorial maps below have a distinguished dart 0 and are quotiented
by orientation-preserving dart relabellings fixing that dart. A rotation system
with Euler characteristic 2 encodes precisely a connected oriented-sphere map.
No simplicity restriction is imposed. Vertices and edges are rotation and
edge-reversal orbits, respectively. Every edge subset is included in the law.
The index n here represents source size n+1, so the limit is through ALL
positive edge sizes, not a subsequence.

MetricMeasureData packages the actual distance, measurable space and measure,
without using arbitrary representatives of measure-preserving isometry classes
as a global typeclass. Valid objects are compact metric probability spaces.
The GHP extended distance is the infimum over common isometric embeddings,
represented by pseudometrics on the disjoint union; zero cross distances simply
identify points in the common ambient space. Tests are bounded functions
continuous for that distance on valid objects. Thus GHP convergence is written
in its bounded-continuous-test (weak distributional convergence) definition.

A standard normalized Brownian excursion is represented in its standard
three-dimensional Bessel-bridge realization: the norm of three independent
standard Brownian bridges of duration 1. No rescaling of the excursion is used.
-/

noncomputable section
open Set Filter MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory
open scoped Topology BigOperators ENNReal NNReal

namespace FKCRT

abbrev Dart (n : ℕ) := Fin (2 * (n + 1))

abbrev Orbit {n : ℕ} (s : Equiv.Perm (Dart n)) :=
  Quotient (Equiv.Perm.SameCycle.setoid s)

def orbit {n : ℕ} (s : Equiv.Perm (Dart n)) (d : Dart n) : Orbit s :=
  Quotient.mk _ d

instance {n : ℕ} (s : Equiv.Perm (Dart n)) : Fintype (Orbit s) := Fintype.ofFinite _

/-- The spanning graph with precisely the edges in A, with loops omitted only
from adjacency: they still exist as edge orbits, have FK weight, and contribute
two darts to the degree measure. Omitting loops does not change distance or k. -/
def spanningGraph {n : ℕ} (a s : Equiv.Perm (Dart n)) (A : Set (Orbit a)) :
    SimpleGraph (Orbit s) :=
  SimpleGraph.fromRel fun v w =>
    ∃ d : Dart n, orbit a d ∈ A ∧ orbit s d = v ∧ orbit s (a d) = w

/-- Labelled connected orientable genus-zero rotation systems. -/
def MapConditions {n : ℕ} (P : Equiv.Perm (Dart n) × Equiv.Perm (Dart n)) : Prop :=
  Function.Involutive P.1 ∧ (∀ d, P.1 d ≠ d) ∧
  (spanningGraph P.1 P.2 Set.univ).Connected ∧
  Nat.card (Orbit P.2) + Nat.card (Orbit (P.2 * P.1)) = n + 3

abbrev LabelledMap (n : ℕ) :=
  {P : Equiv.Perm (Dart n) × Equiv.Perm (Dart n) // MapConditions P}

/-- Root-preserving isomorphism of oriented maps (both dart permutations intertwine). -/
def MapIso {n : ℕ} (M N : LabelledMap n) : Prop :=
  ∃ r : Equiv.Perm (Dart n), r 0 = 0 ∧
    (∀ d, N.val.1 (r d) = r (M.val.1 d)) ∧
    (∀ d, N.val.2 (r d) = r (M.val.2 d))

abbrev RootedMap (n : ℕ) := Quotient (Relation.EqvGen.setoid (@MapIso n))

instance (n : ℕ) : Fintype (RootedMap n) := Fintype.ofFinite _

def reversal {n : ℕ} (M : RootedMap n) : Equiv.Perm (Dart n) := M.out.val.1

def rotation {n : ℕ} (M : RootedMap n) : Equiv.Perm (Dart n) := M.out.val.2

abbrev Vertex {n : ℕ} (M : RootedMap n) := Orbit (rotation M)
abbrev Edge {n : ℕ} (M : RootedMap n) := Orbit (reversal M)

def graph {n : ℕ} (M : RootedMap n) (A : Set (Edge M)) : SimpleGraph (Vertex M) :=
  spanningGraph (reversal M) (rotation M) A

/-- The source k_M(A), including isolated vertices. -/
def clusterCount {n : ℕ} (M : RootedMap n) (A : Finset (Edge M)) : ℕ :=
  Nat.card (graph M (A : Set (Edge M))).ConnectedComponent

/-- Exact decorated finite-map weight from eq:finite-law. -/
def fkWeight {n : ℕ} (q : ℝ) (M : RootedMap n) (A : Finset (Edge M)) : ℝ :=
  Real.rpow q ((clusterCount M A : ℝ) +
    ((A.card : ℝ) - (Nat.card (Vertex M) : ℝ)) / 2)

def partition (q : ℝ) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ M : RootedMap n, ∑ A : Finset (Edge M), fkWeight q M A

/-- Unbundled presentation of a measured metric space; validity is specified below. -/
structure MetricMeasureData where
  Carrier : Type
  distance : Carrier → Carrier → ℝ
  measurableSpace : MeasurableSpace Carrier
  mass : @Measure Carrier measurableSpace

/-- Exactly compact metric probability spaces, with their Borel sigma algebras. -/
def MetricMeasureData.Valid (X : MetricMeasureData) : Prop :=
  ∃ m : MetricSpace X.Carrier,
    letI := m
    (∀ x y, dist x y = X.distance x y) ∧
    CompactSpace X.Carrier ∧ X.measurableSpace = borel X.Carrier ∧
    X.mass Set.univ = 1

/-- Normalized degree measure is uniform dart mass pushed to vertices. -/
def degreeMass {n : ℕ} (M : RootedMap n) :
    @Measure (Vertex M) ⊤ :=
  letI : MeasurableSpace (Vertex M) := ⊤
  ∑ d : Dart n, ENNReal.ofReal (1 / (2 * (n + 1) : ℝ)) •
    Measure.dirac (orbit (rotation M) d)

/-- Root and decoration are not included in the metric probability space. -/
def mapSpace {n : ℕ} (scale : ℝ) (M : RootedMap n) : MetricMeasureData where
  Carrier := Vertex M
  distance v w := scale * ((graph M Set.univ).dist v w : ℝ)
  measurableSpace := ⊤
  mass := degreeMass M

/-- An ambient pseudometric on the disjoint union giving isometric embeddings. -/
def Amalgamates (X Y : MetricMeasureData)
    (m : PseudoMetricSpace (X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier)) : Prop :=
  letI := m
  (∀ x x', dist (Sum.inl x : X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier) (Sum.inl x') = X.distance x x') ∧
  (∀ y y', dist (Sum.inr y : X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier) (Sum.inr y') = Y.distance y y')

def ambientCost (X Y : MetricMeasureData)
    (m : PseudoMetricSpace (X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier)) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  letI := m
  letI : MeasurableSpace (X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier) := borel _
  max (Metric.hausdorffEDist (Set.range (Sum.inl : X.Carrier → X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier))
    (Set.range (Sum.inr : Y.Carrier → X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier)))
    (levyProkhorovEDist
      (@Measure.map X.Carrier (X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier) X.measurableSpace
        (borel _) Sum.inl X.mass)
      (@Measure.map Y.Carrier (X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier) Y.measurableSpace
        (borel _) Sum.inr Y.mass))

/-- The common-isometric-embedding definition of the source GHP distance. -/
def ghpEDist (X Y : MetricMeasureData) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨅ m : {m : PseudoMetricSpace (X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier) // Amalgamates X Y m},
    ambientCost X Y m.val

/-- Bounded continuous tests on compact metric probability spaces modulo
measure-preserving isometry, expressed on presentations. -/
def IsGHPTest (F : MetricMeasureData → ℝ) : Prop :=
  (∃ C : ℝ, ∀ X, |F X| ≤ C) ∧
  ∀ X, X.Valid → ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ δ : ℝ, 0 < δ ∧
    ∀ Y, Y.Valid → ghpEDist X Y < ENNReal.ofReal δ → |F Y - F X| < ε

/-- Source excursion pseudometric, with the closed interval minimum. -/
def excursionDist (e : unitInterval → ℝ) (s t : unitInterval) : ℝ :=
  e s + e t - 2 * sInf (e '' Set.uIcc s t)

abbrev ExcursionTree (e : unitInterval → ℝ) :=
  Quotient (Relation.EqvGen.setoid (fun s t => excursionDist e s t = 0))

def treeProjection (e : unitInterval → ℝ) (t : unitInterval) : ExcursionTree e :=
  Quotient.mk _ t

/-- The quotient by d_e=0, its induced metric, and pushforward of uniform time.
The equivalence closure is redundant for continuous e; the induced distance
is independent of representatives, as required by the pseudometric proof. -/
def treeSpace (e : unitInterval → ℝ) : MetricMeasureData :=
  letI : TopologicalSpace (ExcursionTree e) :=
    TopologicalSpace.coinduced (treeProjection e) inferInstance
  letI : MeasurableSpace (ExcursionTree e) := borel _
  { Carrier := ExcursionTree e
    distance := fun x y => excursionDist e x.out y.out
    measurableSpace := borel _
    mass := Measure.map (treeProjection e) (volume : Measure unitInterval) }

/-- Standard normalized excursion, realized as the 3D Bessel bridge. -/
def standardExcursion {Ω : Type} (B : Fin 3 → ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ) (ω : Ω)
    (t : unitInterval) : ℝ :=
  Real.sqrt (∑ i : Fin 3, (B i ⟨(t : ℝ), t.property.1⟩ ω -
    (t : ℝ) * B i 1 ω) ^ 2)

/-- Exact expectation under the finite law, with n+1 edges. -/
def finiteExpectation (q scale : ℝ) (n : ℕ) (F : MetricMeasureData → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ M : RootedMap n, ∑ A : Finset (Edge M),
    (fkWeight q M A / partition q n) * F (mapSpace scale M)

def brownianExpectation {Ω : Type} [MeasurableSpace Ω] (P : Measure Ω)
    (B : Fin 3 → ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ) (F : MetricMeasureData → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∫ ω, F (treeSpace (standardExcursion B ω)) ∂P

/-- thm:main. The quantifiers over Brownian realizations express that the limit
is the standard excursion-tree law, not a coupling-specific object. -/
def MainStatement : Prop :=
  ∀ q : ℝ, 4 < q → ∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧
    ∀ (Ω : Type) (mΩ : MeasurableSpace Ω) (P : @Measure Ω mΩ),
      @IsProbabilityMeasure Ω mΩ P →
      ∀ B : Fin 3 → ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ,
        (∀ i, @IsBrownianReal Ω mΩ (B i) P) →
        @iIndepFun Ω (Fin 3) mΩ (fun _ => ℝ≥0 → ℝ) (fun _ => inferInstance)
          (fun i ω t => B i t ω) P →
        ∀ F : MetricMeasureData → ℝ, IsGHPTest F →
          Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => finiteExpectation q (c / Real.sqrt (n + 1)) n F)
            atTop (𝓝 (@brownianExpectation Ω mΩ P B F))


/-- Finite FK maps converge in GHP distribution to the standard Brownian CRT. -/
theorem finite_fk_maps_converge_to_brownian_crt : MainStatement := by
  sorry

end FKCRT
end

end OAI
```

      "statement_lean": "def Amalgamates (X Y : MetricMeasureData)\n    (m : PseudoMetricSpace (X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier)) : Prop :=\n  letI := m\n  (∀ x x', dist (Sum.inl x : X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier) (Sum.inl x') = X.distance x x') ∧\n  (∀ y y', dist (Sum.inr y : X.Carrier ⊕ Y.Carrier) (Sum.inr y') = Y.distance y y')"

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the finite-volume continuum-random-tree prediction for critical Fortuin–Kasteleyn planar maps at every fixed <i>q</i> &gt; 4. After rescaling graph distances by a constant times <i>n</i><sup>−1/2</sup>, an <i>n</i>-edge map with normalized degree measure converges to the Brownian continuum random tree in the Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology. The convergence holds through all positive integer sizes.
